金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 10 月 19 日
上週重點訊息
◼ 美國 9 月通膨升溫，Fed 年底前再升息機率仍高： 美國 9 月 CPI 年增 3.7%，高於 8 月的
3.4%；核心 CPI 年增率亦由 2.4%升至 2.6%，顯示通膨壓力不只來自能源，核心價格亦有回
升。隔日的 PPI 年增率則由 5.6%升至 6.3%，反映上游成本壓力仍強。通膨重新升溫，使Fed
即使面對就業降溫，也難以快速轉向寬鬆，市場對年底前再度升息的預期仍高。
◼ 台積電財報創新高，AI 需求強勁但市場轉向檢視融資風險：台積電 10/15 公布其第三季淨利

達 7,631 億元、年增 69%，連續第六季刷新紀錄，第三季美元營收 467.2 億美元，並預估第
四季營收將升至 523～543 億美元 ； 公司同時將2026 全年營收成長預估提高至約 44%， 反映
AI 晶片與先進製程需求仍極為強勁。然而，高利率環境使市場開始檢視 AI 資料中心業者的槓
桿與融資能力，CoreWeave 約 500 億美元債務引發信用疑慮，引發市場波動。
◼ 中東衝突再度推高能源風險 ， 油價高檔加劇全球通膨壓力 ：胡塞武裝上週攻擊沙烏地阿美設施 ，
伊朗亦表示採取新的海上攻擊策略，使荷莫茲海峽與波斯灣航運風險再度升高。10/16 布蘭特
原油與西德洲原油期貨雙雙收在近三週高點。
市場展望
◼ 科技股中期趨勢料將不變 ， 但短線波動勢將加劇 ：近期市場同時面臨通膨升溫 、AI 資本支出疑
慮與重要科技財報公佈在即等不確定因素，短線風險偏好可能受到壓抑。在利率維持高檔及市
場評價偏高的環境下，市場短線料將隨財報結果與市場預期而出現較大波動。然而，上週台積
電與 ASML 財報結果整體優於預期 ， 可見AI 相關企業營運與資本支出尚未出現全面轉弱跡象 ，

科技產業基本面仍具一定支撐，惟後續宜更加重視企業獲利、現金流及資本支出變動及科技子
產業的表現分化現象。
◼ 中天期美債與投資級債債券兼顧息收與利率風險：美國經濟仍具韌性 ， 加上政府與企業發債供
給維持高檔，可能使市場利率持續處於較高水準。雖然投資級債信用利差位於相對低檔，但企
業獲利、現金流及償債能力仍具支撐，加上較高的美國公債基準利率，使投資級債的利息收益
仍具吸引力。相較之下，短天期債券面臨未來利率下降後的再投資風險，長天期債券則較容易
受到公債供給增加 、 期限溢酬及利率波動影響 ， 因此中天期美國公債與投資債券較能兼顧息收
與利率風險。

國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
美國經濟維持溫和擴張，勞動市場降溫與通膨黏著牽動聯準會政策 
經濟 
⚫ 2026 年第 2 季美國實質 GDP 季增年率終值上修至 2.2%， 惟低於第1 季
之 2.5%。成長主要由個人消費及固定投資帶動，其中消費貢獻 2.51 個
百分點；惟進口大增 12.6%，致淨出口拖累 1.10 個百分點。另實質私人
國內最終銷售成長 4.6%，顯示內需仍具韌性。 
⚫ 9 月美國非農就業僅增 2.9 萬人，遠低於前月及近一年平均，失業率升
至 4.2%， 薪資增幅續降 ， 顯示企業聘僱動能轉弱 。 惟勞動市場尚未明顯
惡化，後續仍須關注就業降溫對聯準會利率決策之影響。 
⚫ 8 月美國職位空缺數由 733.5 萬個降至 707.9 萬個，職缺率降至 4.3%，
顯示企業徵才需求略為降溫；惟聘僱人數增至 519.2 萬人。主動離職及
裁員規模同步下降，反映勞工轉職意願與企業人力調整趨於審慎，勞動
市場供需大致維持平衡。 
⚫ 9 月美國 ISM 製造業採購經理人指數為 54.5，連續第九個月維持擴張。
新訂單、就業及未完成訂單同步改善，顯示需求仍具韌性；惟生產、出
口及進口動能略為放緩，且原物料成本持續攀升，加上關稅政策、地緣
政治及供應鏈不確定性仍存，製造業仍面臨部分壓力。 
⚫ 8 月美國 PCE 物價指數月增 0.3%、年增 3.4%，核心 PCE 月增 0.2%、
年增 3.0%， 顯示通膨大致持穩但短期物價壓力略升 。 另一方面 ， 個人消
費支出月增 0.9%、實質消費成長 0.6%，帶動儲蓄率降至 4.1%，顯示家
庭需求仍具韌性；後續須關注消費熱度及通膨對聯準會政策之影響。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 近期聯準會官員立場趨於分歧，理事 Michael Barr 認為能源價格及 AI
投資熱潮可能延緩通膨降溫 ， 未來仍可能進一步升息 ； 惟John Williams、
Philip Jefferson 及 Michelle Bowman 等官員則主張暫無急於升息的必要 ，
傾向觀察後續經濟數據。加以 9 月非農就業明顯放緩，市場對 10 月升
息預期大幅降溫，焦點轉向 12 月會議。 
⚫ 台積電傳評估與馬斯克旗下 Terafab 合作赴德州設廠，雙方仍處討論階
段。合作可能由台積電主導營運，或由 Terafab 持多數股權、台積電提
供技術 。 若成真 ， 將擴大台積電美國布局 ， 並支援Tesla、SpaceX 及 xAI
晶片需求。

短評 
⚫ 美國經濟目前仍維持溫和擴張，內需與投資表現優於預期，短期衰退風
險有限 ； 惟近期新增就業明顯低於預期 ， 企業聘僱意願與職缺需求放緩 ，
勞動市場動能已有轉弱跡象，而通膨受能源價格、 AI 投資及關稅等因
素影響仍具黏著性。在經濟成長放緩與通膨尚未完全受控的環境下，聯
準會短期可能傾向維持觀望，惟若通膨再度升溫，不排除年底前仍有進
一步升息空間。 
歐 
元 
區 
法國殖利率大幅上升，不利於投資及消費表現 
經濟 
⚫ 歐元區 9 月 HICP 通膨、核心通膨(不含食品及能源)分別上升 0.6、0.1
個百分點至 3.8%、2.5%。 
⚫ 歐元區 8 月失業率持平 6.4%。 
⚫ 歐元區第二季家庭儲蓄率下降 0.2 個百分點至 14.2%。 
⚫ 歐元區第二季房價年增率為 4%，較第一季降 0.6 個百分點。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 法國 10 年期公債殖利率升至 4.9%以上，與德國公債殖利率差距擴大至
125 個基點以上。 
短評 ⚫ 法國財政前景不明朗 ， 未來仍可能面臨殖利率進一步上升與財政壓力加劇的風險，暫時不利於消費及投資表現。 
日 
本 
景氣溫和復甦，但消費放緩與通膨升溫風險升高 
經濟 
⚫ 8 月工業生產月減 1.7%。 
⚫ 8 月零售月減 1.2%。 
⚫ 9 月東京核心通膨年增 2.7%。 
短評 
⚫ 儘管消費動能放緩與通膨壓力升溫 ， 但企業投資與生產回升趨勢仍具韌
性，經濟可望維持溫和復甦，惟日本央行進一步升息的可能性正逐步提
高。 
中 
國 
為刺激內需、引導產業走向，人行漸近式放寬政策工具 
經濟 ⚫ 9 月官方製造業 PMI、非製造業 PMI 分別上升為 50.1、50.2。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 人行於 9 月 29 日宣布下調抵押補充貸款(該貸款本質是將資金引導至政
府希望發展的領域)利率自 1.75%降至 1.50%，並擴大適用範圍。 
短評 
⚫ 未來若內需復甦力道不如預期 ， 不排除人行將進一步降準或擴大結構性
貨幣工具支持經濟 ； 至於全面降息則仍須兼顧人民幣匯率及中美利差等
因素，可能性相對較低。

國際經濟金融週報 
兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
115.10.5 
一、 美國—美國經濟維持溫和擴張，勞動市場降溫與通膨黏著牽動聯準會政策 
2026 年第二季美國實質 GDP 季增年率終值由前次估計之 1.5%上修至 2.2%，
惟仍低於第一季之 2.5%， 顯示經濟持續擴張但成長動能略有放緩 。 此次上修主要
反映投資、消費及政府支出優於原先估計。就成長結構觀察，個人消費支出仍為
主要動能，貢獻 2.51 個百分點，其中服務及商品消費分別貢獻 1.57 及 0.94 個百
分點；私人投資貢獻 0.82 個百分點，以設備投資及智慧財產權投資表現強勁，惟
庫存調整拖累 0.53 個百分點。對外貿易方面，出口成長 5.0%，但進口大幅增加
12.6%， 致淨出口拖累GDP 成長 1.10 個百分點 。 另反映內需基本面的實質私人國
內最終銷售成長 4.6%， 高於第一季之1.8%， 顯示民間需求明顯增強 。 物價方面 ，
第 2 季 PCE 及核心 PCE 物價指數分別上升 5.0%及 3.3%， 顯示通膨壓力仍偏高 。
整體而言，美國經濟仍由消費與固定投資支撐，惟進口快速增加及通膨黏著性，
仍為後續經濟前景的重要觀察重點。 
2026 年 9 月美國非農就業人數增加 2.9 萬人 ， 明顯低於上月修正後的13.3 萬
人，也低於過去 12 個月平均每月增加 4.5 萬人的水準；失業率小幅升至 4.2%，
整體仍維持在 3 月以來 4.1%至 4.3%的區間，反映勞動市場整體變化有限，惟新
增就業力道已轉趨疲弱。家庭調查方面，勞動參與率升至 61.8%，就業人口比為
59.2%，均較上月小幅上升；長期失業人數約 194.4 萬人，占總失業人口 27.1%，
大致維持穩定。產業別方面，主要產業就業變化有限，其中醫療保健增加 1.7 萬
人、營建業增加 1.1 萬人、製造業增加 0.9 萬人，金融活動則減少 0.7 萬人，政府
部門減少 1.7 萬人。薪資方面，私人部門平均時薪升至 37.81 美元，月增 0.1%、
年增 3.0%，平均每週工時維持 34.4 小時。另 7 月與 8 月新增就業合計下修 6 萬
人，反映前二月就業動能較初估疲弱。整體而言，9 月美國新增就業明顯放緩、
失業率小幅上升且薪資增速降溫，企業聘僱動能較先前轉弱；惟失業率仍維持於
近期區間，勞動市場尚未出現明顯惡化跡象，後續仍須關注企業聘僱需求是否持
續降溫，以及對聯準會利率決策的影響。 
2026 年 8 月美國職位空缺數(Job Openings) 由上月修正後之 733.5 萬個減少
25.6 萬個至 707.9 萬個 ， 職缺率由4.4%降至 4.3%， 顯示企業用人需求略有降溫 ；

惟聘僱人數由 514.6 萬人小幅增加至 519.2 萬人，聘僱率由 3.2%升至 3.3%。總離
職人數由 512.8 萬人減少至 507.0 萬人，離職率維持於 3.2%。就離職結構觀察，
主動離職(Quits)人數由 308.9 萬人降至 306.6 萬人，主動離職率維持 1.9%；裁員
與解僱(Layoffs and Discharges) 人數則由 170.2 萬人降至 164.1 萬人，裁員率由
1.1%降至 1.0%。產業別方面，職位空缺增加主要來自零售業、金融保險業、資訊
業及休閒餐飲業 ； 聘僱人數則以政府部門及製造業增幅較為明顯 。 整體而言 ，8 月
美國職缺數較前月下滑，反映企業徵才需求略有降溫，但聘僱活動仍維持穩定，
且主動離職與裁員規模同步下降，顯示勞工轉職意願及企業人力調整趨於審慎，
勞動市場供需仍大致維持平衡。 
2026 年 9 月美國 ISM 製造業採購經理人指數(Manufacturing PMI) 為 54.5，
較上月之 54.6 微降 0.1 點，惟已連續第九個月維持於 50 以上擴張區間，顯示製
造業景氣持續擴張。細項觀察，新訂單指數由53.7 升至 55.3，需求動能續增；生
產指數由 58.3 降至 56.7，雖仍維持擴張，但成長速度略有放緩；就業指數由51.2
升至 52.7，反映企業聘僱需求持續增加。供應商交貨指數由 59.3 微降至 59.0，交
貨延遲情況仍存；庫存指數則由 50.6 降至 48.6，企業備貨態度轉趨保守。此外，
價格指數由 71.1 升至 77.9，原物料及投入成本壓力升高；未完成訂單指數由51.8
升至 56.4，訂單去化變慢。另一方面，新出口訂單指數由53.2 降至 50.9，進口指
數亦由 52.5 降至 51.0，外需成長動能及企業備料需求略為放緩。整體而言，9 月
美國製造業活動持續穩健擴張，新訂單、就業及未完成訂單同步改善，顯示製造
業景氣仍具韌性；惟出口及進口增速放緩、投入成本壓力升高，加上關稅政策、
地緣政治風險及供應鏈不確定性仍存，後續發展仍值得持續關注。 
2026 年 8 月美國個人消費支出(PCE)物價指數月增率由上月之 0.1%升至 0.3%，
年增率維持於 3.4%； 扣除食品及能源後之核心PCE 月增率由 0.1%升至 0.2%，年
增率亦持平於 3.0%，顯示整體通膨大致持穩，惟短期物價漲勢較前月略有回升。
另一方面，個人所得月增 0.2%，可支配所得(DPI)月增 0.3%，實質可支配所得持
平；個人消費支出月增 0.9%，實質個人消費支出成長 0.6%，顯示民間消費表現
明顯回溫。由於消費增幅高於所得增幅，個人儲蓄率由前月之 4.6%降至 4.1%。
整體而言，8 月美國通膨表現大致持穩，但消費支出顯著回升，反映家庭需求仍
具韌性；後續仍須持續關注消費熱度、通膨走勢及其對聯準會貨幣政策路徑之影
響。

近期聯準會官員談話呈現鷹鴿分歧。聯準會理事 Michael Barr 認為，中東局
勢推升能源價格，以及 AI 相關投資熱潮帶動需求增長，均可能阻礙通膨回落至
2%目標 ， 未來仍不排除進一步升息的必要性 ； 惟 紐約聯準銀行總裁 John Williams、
聯準會副主席 Philip Jefferson 及金融監理副主席 Michelle Bowman 則強調，在 9
月升息後，當前並無急迫性立即進一步收緊政策，應先觀察後續通膨、就業及金
融市場數據變化，再評估是否有進一步調整利率的必要。受官員談話轉趨審慎，
以及 9 月非農就業表現疲弱影響，市場對 10 月 FOMC 會議再度升息的預期明顯
降溫，政策焦點逐漸轉向 12 月會議及年底前是否仍有一次升息空間。 
晶圓代工龍頭台積電傳出正評估與馬斯克旗下 Terafab 合作，在美國德州布
局晶圓廠，馬斯克也於社群平台 X 證實雙方正在討論，並表示 「目前僅在討論階
段 ， 但可能會有進展」 。 目前浮上檯面的合作模式包括由台積電持有並營運新廠 ，
SpaceX 或 Terafab 透過入股及承諾採購晶圓參與；另一方案則由 Terafab 掌握多
數股權 ， 台積電提供製程技術與晶圓廠營運經驗 。Terafab 旨在因應 Tesla、SpaceX
及 xAI 快速成長的 AI 晶片需求，首期投資規模達 168 億美元。若合作成真，不
僅可望成為台積電繼亞利桑那州後在美國的新布局，也凸顯先進製程與穩定晶片
供應的重要性，但目前具體合作方式、時程與投資內容仍未正式定案。 
二、歐元區—法國殖利率大幅上升，不利於投資及消費表現 
 歐元區 9 月 HICP 通膨 、 核心通膨(不含食品及能源)分別上升 0.6、0.1 個百分
點至 3.8%、2.5%， 主因國際原油價格上漲 ， 使能源通膨上升4.5 個百分點至 18.8%，
並透過肥料、物流成本上漲，間接推升食品通膨 0.3 個百分點至 1.4%。另外，服
務通膨上升 0.2 個百分點至 3.2%，近三個月服務通膨大致呈現橫向波動，部分反
映旅遊旺季因素影響。基於布蘭特原油持續位於每桶 90 美元以上，預期年底前
仍將對通膨帶來額外壓力，後續須關注能源漲價可能會透過運輸、物流等成本管
道傳導至核心商品及服務價格，進而提高核心通膨黏性。 
 其他經濟數據包含 ：(1)歐元區 8 月失業率持平 6.4%， 失業人數增加2.6 萬人 ，
主要是義大利勞動參與人數增加所致，不過 ，基於PMI 顯示近期德國勞動雇用需
求持續增溫，將有助於穩定勞動市場表現；(2)歐元區第二季家庭儲蓄率下降 0.2
個百分點至 14.2%，顯示通膨壓力削弱民眾購買力，致儲蓄暫時性減少；(3)歐元
區第二季房價年增率為 4%， 較第一季降0.6 個百分點 ， 主因法國中古屋面臨嚴格

環保法規，須負擔高昂翻修成本，使法國房價呈現連三季下滑所拖累。 
 近一週法國 10 年期公債殖利率升至 4.9%以上，與德國公債殖利率差距擴大
至 125 個基點以上，主要反映市場對法國主權公債可持續性疑慮升溫。儘管目前
政府計畫將 2027 年財政赤字縮限至 5%，惟政府在國會並無穩定多數席次，即使
能以行政命令強行通過預算 ， 也可能面臨倒閣風險 ；此外 ，市場對於2027 年 4 月
總統大選後，新總統能否取得國會足夠支持，推動削減赤字與控制債務的改革亦
缺乏信心。因此未來法國仍可能面臨殖利率進一步上升與財政壓力加劇的風險，
暫時不利於投資及消費信心。 
三、日本—景氣溫和復甦，但消費放緩與通膨升溫風險升高 
 8 月工業生產月減 1.7%，主要係受到該月地震及豪雨等天然災害影響，導致
部分工廠暫時停產。從細項來看，汽車業受停工影響最為明顯，產量下滑6.8%，
電子產品產量亦減少 1.9%。 惟生產機械產量連續三個月成長 ， 加上核心資本財出
貨持續增加，顯示企業投資需求仍相當強勁，有助於支撐後續工業生產回升。從
企業預期來看，製造商預期9 月產量將成長 3.2%，顯示生產活動可望迅速回溫，
在供應鏈逐步恢復、庫存回補需求持續，以及穩健的企業資本支出支撐下，預計
第三季工業生產仍可維持正成長，且成長動能可望延續至年底。 
 8 月零售月減 1.2%，雖然表現略優於市場預期，但仍顯示民間消費動能正在
放緩。此次疲弱主要原因來自耐久財銷售下滑，其中家電銷售因空調等產品在換
機需求告一段落後明顯回落，汽車銷售也隨政府刺激措施效應逐漸消退而連續第
四個月下降。相較之下，食品、服裝等非耐久財銷售雖同步走弱，但降幅相對有
限 ， 反映薪資成長仍對消費提供部分支撐 。惟不利天候與通膨壓力仍是主要風險 ，
8 月至 9 月持續豪雨已影響服裝等非必需消費，而除米價以外的食品及商品價格
持續上漲，也正侵蝕家庭實質購買力，恐限制消費者信心與消費復甦力道。 
 9 月東京核心通膨年增率升至 2.7%，雖然東京都免費托兒及免費水費政策造
成的基期效應貢獻約 0.5 個百分點，但通膨升溫主因仍是價格上漲已由能源與食
品擴散至整體經濟。受石油價格上揚帶動，企業開始加速將原物料及薪資成本轉
嫁給消費者，帶動商品與服務價格普遍走高；租金通膨更升至 2%，創 1994 年以
來新高，公共交通及醫療等項目也開始調漲。隨著東京的物價壓力逐步反映至全
國數據，日本央行進一步升息的可能性升高。

四、中國—為刺激內需、引導產業走向，人行漸近式放寬政策工具 
9 月官方製造業 PMI 上升 0.3 點至 50.1，為近三個月高點，新訂單、出口訂
單指數分別為 50.5、50，並帶動生產指數上升至 51.7，不過，庫存指數下降、就
業指數加速衰退、企業展望減弱，皆拖累 PMI 指數水準。此外，投入、產出物價
指數分別上升至 60.8、54，顯示成本壓力加劇，但售價調整速度依然較為遲緩，
不利於獲利表現。官方非製造業 PMI 上升 1.2 點至 50.2，其中建築業活動指數大
增 3.4 點至 50.3，脫離連續八個月的衰退區間，主要是政府加大財政刺激力度，
推動重大建設開工；非製造業的從業人員指數為 45.9，顯示就業情況仍顯嚴峻，
亦較不利於內需表現。 
鑒於內需動能疲弱，人行於 9 月 29 日宣布下調抵押補充貸款(該貸款本質是
將資金引導至政府希望發展的領域)利率自 1.75%降至 1.50%，並擴大適用範圍，
將水網、新型電網、算力網、新一代通信網、城市地下管網及物流網等建設納入
支持，有助於提振內需動能。未來若內需復甦力道不如預期，不排除人行將進一
步降準或擴大結構性貨幣工具支持經濟；至於全面降息則仍須兼顧人民幣匯率及
中美利差等因素，可能性相對較低。 
五、印度及東南亞—泰國、菲律賓出口均達 2 成以上的高速成長 
(1)新加坡：受服務業、零售暨其他商品業，以及食品價格漲幅擴大影響，8 月消

條件不佳影響，預期新加坡進口食品、商品及服務成本將持續上升，惟國內生產
率成長及政府補貼政策將抑制部分服務業通膨，新加坡金管局(MAS)或在 10 月
「略微」緊縮貨幣政策。(2)泰國：8 月出口額年增 24.3%(346 億美元)，主要由工

運輸設備（13.6%）為主。進出口相抵，8 月貿易逆差 24.8 億美元，較 7 月略收
窄，為連續第 11 個月出現逆差。(3)菲律賓：8 月出口額大增 27.8%(91 億美元)，
創兩年新高，主要得益於電子產品（年增 59.9%） ，尤其是半導體（73.5%）及黃

### Page 1

ab 15 October 2026
Global Research
Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing
Full speed on capacity expansion, backed by a
strong US commitment
Cloud AI demand strengthening, with no signs of slowing
TSMC management remains optimistic of a stronger multi-year Cloud AI mega-trend. Its
optimism is based on its prudent and comprehensive assessment of data centre
construction, power shell build up, component supply, and the financial situation of
Cloud service operators. Its capex target was retained at US$60-64bn in 2026, implying

a meaningful uptick to US$19.4-23.4bn in Q4 vs US$40.6bn in 9M26. Our recent
research highlighted TSMC is further accelerating expansion across N3/N2/A14, and
could lift capex to US$90bn in 2027E and US$105bn in 2028E.
Stronger commitment to  US expansion
TSMC guided the overseas dilution on gross margin to rise to 3-4% in 2027 from a
narrowing to 2% (vs. prior 2-3%) in 2026 given the accelerating expansion especially in
the US. The second fab in Arizona should start mass production for N3 in Q327, slightly

earlier than prior plan. Management also stated that it's in talk with SpaceX/Terafab on
potential collaboration, with key principle being to operate the fabs by TSMC.  As what
we wrote recently, TSMC is also evaluating the possibility to expand outside  Arizona,
with Texas being an option. The greater ambition to expand global footprint and
support reshoring is key in solidifying TSMC's competitive positioning, in our view.
Better Q4 sales guidance, slightly down GM guidance not concerning

Q3 EPS of NT$29.43 was above LSEG consensus of NT$28.67, mostly due to higher sales
and OPM of 58.7%. Q3 gross margin of 66.4% was a bit below expectation of 67%
into earnings, given N2 dilution. Q4 sales is guided to grow 14% QoQ at mid point,
slightly ahead of expectation of ~10% QoQ into earnings, driving full year sales
guidance in USD to grow 44% YoY. TSMC guided greater dilution from N2 and overseas
expansion, which lowers Q4 GM 40bps QoQ to 66.0% at the mid point of guidance. We

view TSMC’s investment case on strong growth at sustainable margins with good
support in 2027  (UBS GMs up 10bps YoY at 66.7% from 66.6% in 2026) with price
hikes to pass on higher costs.
Valuation: maintain Buy and target of NT$3,650
We raise 2026-28E EPS by 2-3% to reflect higher sales even with slightly lower gross
margin assumptions. Our new 2028E EPS is 10% above consensus pre-earnings. We
view resilient risk/reward for TSMC stock at 16x 2027E PE vs 36% earnings growth in

2027 based on UBSe, and expect the stock to steadily outperform the broader tech
sector at least through early 2027, assuming the market volatility persists. Reiterate Buy
rating. Our price target is based on 20x 2027-28E PE with a 26% long-term earnings
CAGR.

Company Description
Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing (TSMC) is the world's leading dedicated foundry
service provider, with annual capacity of about 32m of 8-inch equivalent wafers as of end-
2024. It operates four 12-inch wafer fabs, four 8-inch wafer fabs and one 6-inch wafer fab in

Taiwan. TSMC also manages two 8-inch fabs and one 12-inch fab overseas under wholly
owned subsidiaries WaferTech in the US, and TSMC China and TSMC Nanjing in China.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We base our price target for TSMC on a PE multiple.
We think TSMC faces a variety of risks, including rapidly changing technology, intense
competition, high capital investment and cyclical demand. TSMC also has a history of paying
stock options to its employees, which could dilute earnings.

KEY DEFINITIONS:Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is defined as expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over
the next 12 months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA) is defined as
the one-year local market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR)
Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the

near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have an investment
horizon of 12 months.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

Update
Co-Tech Development Corp.  | Asia Pacific
Announces additional
investment in Malaysia
subsidiary
What’s new? Co-Tech’s board today approved an additional NT$1.4bn investment in
its 100%-owned subsidiary, CoTech Development (Penang) Sdn. Bhd., to be financed
with internal funds or bank loans. This follows the company’s September 14
announcement that it would establish a subsidiary in Southeast Asia (note).
Our view: Our latest checks suggest that, due to strong demand from key

customers, the Malaysia plant will primarily produce advanced HVLP copper foil
rather than the RG-series foil originally planned. The new plant is also likely to be
larger than Yunlin Plant III, where Co-Tech plans to add ~600 tons of HVLP3/4
capacity by end-2027. Volume production in Malaysia is unlikely to start before late
2028, so most of the ramp-up should fall in 2029, contributing to that year’s
earnings. On funding, our checks also suggest Co-Tech will finance further

investment in the new plant’s construction and equipment by issuing convertible
bonds.
See also our initiation report: Asia Pacific Technology: AI’s Hidden Backbone (13 Sep
2026)

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Co-Tech Development Corp. (8358.TWO)
Base case, derived from a residual income (RI) model. Key assumptions include a cost of equity
of 9.3% (beta of 1.0, equity premium of 8.7% and risk-free rate of 1%), medium-term growth
rate of 13%, and terminal growth rate of 3%.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger AI demand
n Slower-than-expected capacity expansion by competitors
n Faster-than-expected material upgrades
n Slower-than-expected CPO adoption

Risks to Downside
n Weaker AI demand
n Faster-than-expected capacity expansion by competitors
n Slower-than-expected material upgrades
n Faster-than-expected CPO adoption

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

營收走勢圖

研究員/游周翰 Jeffery.Yu@uni-psg.com

研究部重申華星光買進建議，主要考量：(1)管理層維持下半年營運優
於上半年展望，受惠 800G DCI 及 CW Laser 需求持續成長，4Q26 單月
營收有望挑戰 6 億元；(2)隨晶粒稼動率及良率持續改善，研究部上修
2027 年晶粒出貨量預估，加上 EML 等產品逐步放量，有望帶動產品組
合優化及獲利能力提升。綜上所述，研究部預估華星光 2026/2027 年
營收，由原先 60.6 億元/119.8 億元調整為 58.4 億元/120.8 億元
(+33%/107%YoY)，EPS 由原先 7.69 元/15.88 元調整為 7.5 元/16.35
元。考量2027 年產能擴充及相關產品出貨成長，營收與獲利有望呈現
倍數成長，維持目標價 800 元的買進建議。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26營收優於研究部預期，惟受匯率影響EPS僅符合預期： 
華星光於 10/14 公 佈 自 結 ，9 月營收 5.75 億元（ +5.3%MoM 、
+97.8%YoY） ，稅後淨利1.03億元，稅後淨利率達17.9%，EPS達
0.72元。相較於8月回升，除了匯率回穩外，亦受到營收規模放
大所影響 。 累計3Q26營收為16.29億元 （+26.4%QoQ，+51.4%YoY），
稅後淨利達3.26億元，EPS為2.28元，創歷史新高。若依研究部
過去費用率及業外假設回推，3Q26毛利率約28-29%（約+6ppts 
QoQ） ，主要受惠先前財稅認列時間差回補、產能利用率提升及
產品組合優化 。 整體而言 ，3Q26營收優於研究部預期16.19億元 ，
EPS僅符合預期2.26元 ， 推測主因受到匯率所影響毛利率及業外
損益。

表一、3Q26 研究部預估與實際差異 
單位：百萬元 實際 研究部原估 差異 
營收 1,630 1,613 +1% 
稅前淨利 394 417 -5.5% 
稅後淨利 326 333 -2.2% 
EPS(元) 2.28 2.34 -2.6% 
資料來源： 華星光自結、統一研究部預估

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

2. 下半年營運優於上半年，惟毛利率受新產品良率等因素影響 ： 
展望4Q26， 管理層維持2H26營運優於1H26， 除了既有DCI產品持
續成長外，CW Laser需求持續提升、800G DCI進入批量生產，
以及EML與高增益APD開始貢獻營收，有望帶動整體營運向上，
單月營收有望挑戰6億元 。 近期經研究部通路訪查 ，800GZR出貨
量略低於原先預估，因此研究部下修 4Q26出貨量從75K下修至
55K，4Q26營收從原先預估19.6億元下修至17.31億元。此外考
量800GZR毛利率因良率及協助客戶購買稀釋毛利率不如原先研
究部預期的情況下，將EPS從2.51元下修至2.29元。

3. 隨產能擴充，營收獲利將持續成長： 
展望未來，在2027年產能倍增情況下，營收規模有望呈現倍數
成長。晶粒方面，除了既有的CW Laser產能擴充外，亦增加EML
晶粒產能，使公司在InP產品線有多種產品分散風險。此外，近
期經供應鏈訪查，晶粒稼動率及良率提升下，有望使整體晶粒
出貨量優於原先預期。因此研究部預估2027年整體晶粒出貨量
從4700萬顆上修至5000萬顆。綜上所述，研究部預估營收2027
年營收從119.8億元上修至120.8億元 ， EPS從15.88元微幅上修
至16.35元。

資料來源： 華星光自結、統一研究部預估

評等  定義  
強力買進     預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬超過 25%以上  
買進  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬 10% 以上  
中立  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 10～-10%  
降低持股  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 (-10%)以下  
未評等  沒有足夠的基本資料判斷該公司評等

기업분석 2025.09.28 www.daishin.com

Issue & News 
SK하이닉스  
(000660) 
 소음보다 방향성에 
류형근

hyungkeun.ryu@daishin.com

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 목표주가 3,200,000 
유지  
현재주가 
(26.09.23) 
1,862,000 
반도체업종

◼ 최근 시장은 2가지 Risk를 제기. 다만, 과도한 우려는 기우. 
◼ 현 주가는 보다 강인해지는 Fundamental의 가치를 미반영한 수준. 
◼ 비중 확대 전략 유효. 목표주가와 매수의견 유지.

투자전략: 업황을 대변하지 못하고 있는 주가 
목표주가와 매수의견 유지. 현 주가는 업황의 강세를 충분히 반영하지 못하
고 있다고 생각. 1) 메모리반도체 사이클의 추가 확장 (구매 경쟁 심화에 따른 
단기 가격 상승 폭 확대, 기대치를 상회할 4Q26 DRAM 판매량), 2) 3Q26 실
적발표에서 보다 구체화될 주주환원 정책의 방향성에 보다 집중할 필요. 
시장에서 제기하는 Risk가 있다면, 2가지. 1) 주요 고객사향 HBM4 품질 이슈, 
2) Solidigm IPO와 해외 공장 투자에 따른 경계심리 확대가 그 대상. 당사는 
해당 Risk가 Fundamental을 저해할 것으로 보고 있지 않으며, 단기 노이즈보
다는 사이클 확장의 선순환에 보다 주목할 필요가 있다고 생각. 
1. HBM4 품질 이슈, 과도한 우려는 기우 
3Q26부터 HBM4 출하가 본격화될 것이라는 기존 입장을 유지. HBM4 비중
은 3Q26 전체 판매량의 40%, 2027년 연간 50% 중반까지 상승할 것. 
일각에서는 주요 고객사향 HBM4 품질 이슈로 사업 성과가 기대치를 하회할 
수 있다는 우려를 표출. 다만, 과도한 우려는 기우. 후공정 단에서 문제가 일
부 발생했을 수 있으나, 해결 불가능한 Risk라 보기 어렵고, 불량에 따른 배
상금을 논할 단계도 아니라는 판단. 
2. 해외 공장 투자, 실보다 득이 크다 
언론보도에 따르면, SK하이닉스는 해외 공장 투자와 Solidigm IPO를 추진 중. 
해당 결정이 현실화될 경우, 시장은 단기적으로 이를 부정적으로 받아들일 
가능성이 존재. 다만, 결과적으로, 실보다 득이 큰 선택이 될 것이라 생각. 
- 해외 투자가 현실화되더라도, 실질 생산 기여 시점은 2028년 이후일 가능
성이 높음. 미국의 건설 경기와 공장 구축의 세부 단계 등을 감안할 필요. 
- 해외 투자 결정이 관세 Risk를 축소시킬 수 있는 변화라는 점도 감안할 필
요. “불확실성 해소”는 Upcycle의 중반부에서 반도체 주식에 대한 좋은 투자
포인트가 되어왔고, 이번에도 다르지 않을 것이라는 판단.

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 분기 영업이익 100조 시대가 열렸다삼성전자(005930) 분기 영업이익 100조 시대가 열렸다분기 영업이익 100조 시대가 열렸다 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-02
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
26년 3분기 환율 부진에도 영업이익 100조는 넘긴다 
삼성전자 26년 3분기 실적은 이전 전망치를 하회할 전망이다. 매출액은 195.2조원으
로 이전 분기 대비 13.8% 증가하고, 영업이익은 101.7조원으로 13.6% 증가할 것으
로 예상한다. 영업이익률은 52.1%로 전 분기 대비 0.1%p 하락할 전망이다. 원/달러 
환율 영향이 주요 변수로 작용한 것으로 추정한다. 2분기에 집행되지 않았던 성과급 
일부가 3분기에 반영된 영향도 있을 것으로 예상한다. DS 부문은 매출액 149.9조원, 
영업이익은 101.8조원으로 추정하고, 디스플레이 매출액 8.8조원, 영업이익 0.9조
원, MX/네트워크는 매출액 34.3조원, 영업적자 1.1조원으로 예상한다. VD/가전도 
영업적자 규모가 커질 것으로 예상한다.  
주주환원은 주가 상승을 견인할 것 
8월 21일 이후 주주환원에 대한 관심이 커졌다. 자사주 규모는 크지 않겠지만 배당
을 통한 주주환원 규모는 사상 최대 규모일 것으로 예상한다. 글로벌 대표 IT업체와 
비교해도 손색없는 수준이다. 메모리 업체의 주주환원 규모가 100조원 넘게 집행된 
적이 없어서 주가에 어떠한 영향을 미칠 지가 관전 포인트인데 긍정적 역할을 할 것
으로 기대한다. 자사주를 통한 EPS 개선은 아니지만, 안정적이고 지속적인 규모의 
주주환원은 주가를 한층 더 높은 수준에서 평가 받는 계기가 될 것으로 판단한다.  
현저한 저평가 구간, 이전 전망 유지 
삼성전자에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 메모리 공급 부족 상황이 장기화될 
것으로 전망하고, 이로 인해 수익성은 현재 수준에서 유지되고, 이익 규모는 안정적
으로 성장할 것으로 기대하고, 현저히 저평가 국면에 있다고 판단하기 때문이다. 목
표주가는 460,000원을 유지한다.

### Page 4

삼성전자 (005930)

4 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
반도체 Check Point 
1. 차별화된 주주환원 전략. 배경은 안정적 이익 규모 
2026년에는 대대적인 규모의 주주환원이 예상된다. 100 조원 내외 수준으로 
예상하는데 상당 부분은 배당으로 집행될 것으로 추정한다. 배당성향이 25%가 유지될 
것으로 예상하기 때문이다. 자사주 매입 여력은 20조원 내외 수준일 것으로 예상한다. 
주주환원은 주가 밸류 상승에 기여할 것으로 기대한다. 일회성이 아니고 장기적으로 
높은 수준의 주주환원이 가능하다는 것은 싸이클 변동에 따른 이익의 변동성이 
낮다는 의미이기도 하다. 
2. AI 모델 개발 속도 조절. 그래도 메모리 수요를 줄일 수는 없다 
Anthropic CEO는 현재 AI 개발 속도가 지나치게 빨라서 인간의 능력을 뛰어 넘는 
임계점에 이를 가능성이 높아져서 개발 속도를 늦출 필요가 있다고 언급했다. 이를 
Meta, Google, SpaceX 수장들이 공감했다. 우선 이들의 공감으로 AI 모델의 개발 
속도가 둔화될 것으로 보고 있지 않다. 또한, 이를 가정하더라도 향후 AI 모델은 
Agent AI로 진화할 것으로 보고 있다. 이에 따른 메모리에 대한 필요성은 급격히 
증가할 것으로 기대한다. 
3. 자신감 넘치는 HBM  
HBM4가 2026년 하반기부터 주력 제품이 될 것으로 전망한다. 26 년 3분기에는 
비중이 40% 내외, 4 분기에는 60%를 상회할 것으로 예상한다. 연초 기대 대비로는 
낮은 수준이다. 삼성전자의 점유율만 보면 연초 대비 높은 수준의 점유율이 예상된다.  
HBM4 물량이 예상보다 늦게 본격화되면서 2026년 HBM 시장 규모는 이전 전망 
대비 축소될 전망이다. HBM4도 늦은 출시로 연내 생산 물량은 이전 전망치인 130억 
Gb을 하회할 것으로 예상한다.  
2027년 HBM4 가격이 최근에 확정이 되었고, 이를 통해서 DRAM 수준의 수익성 
확보가 가능할 것으로 기대한다. 수익성 발목을 잡았던 HBM은 2027년 물량부터는 
영업이익 개선의 변수가 될 것으로 전망한다.

장기적 주주환원은 
안정적 이익이 뒷받침 
Agent AI는 메모리 
수요를 증가시킨다 
HBM4 안정적 진입한 
것으로 판단

Document 218: AIがサイバー攻撃を自動化する時代、サイバー株は上がるのか ～攻撃能力の急伸と企業業績・株価から検証する～

Source PDF: all report pdf/AIがサイバー攻撃を自動化する時代、サイバー株は上がるのか ～攻撃能力の急伸と企業業績・株価から検証する～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

Research Report

2026 年 10 月 6 日

1/6 
AI がサイバー攻撃を自動化する時代、サイバー株
は上がるのか 
～攻撃能力の急伸と企業業績・株価から検証する～ 
 資産運用調査部 主席研究員  柏村 祐

（要旨） 
○ AI によるサイバー攻撃の自動化・高度化が現実のものとなっている。豪政府システムへのAI
侵入事件などが示すように、AI は与えられた目的を達成する過程で、許可された範囲を超え
てシステムにアクセスしうることが明らかになった。国内でも 9 月下旬以降、幅広い業種の
企業で不正アクセスの公表が相次いでいる。 
○ AI エージェントのサイバー課題解決能力はここ数年で急速に向上しており、サイバーセキュ
リティ企業の売上高・利益も足元で好調に推移している。 
○ 一方で、高度な AI モデルの登場は既存のサイバーセキュリティ製品の陳腐化リスクへの懸
念を生み、関連株が下落するというパラドックスも起きている。「サイバー株なら何でも上
がる」という安易な連想買いには注意が必要である。 
○ 個人投資家は、サイバーセキュリティセクター全体を丸ごと買うのではなく、「AIネイティ
ブ」なサイバーセキュリティ企業や、AIを活用した防衛技術を持つ巨大テック企業（メガテ
ック）への選別投資が求められる。

1. 豪政府システムへの「AI侵入事件」が鳴らす警鐘 
2026 年9月、オーストラリアのアルバニージー首相は、同国の公的医療保険制度（Medicare）
の統計ポータルサイトに、OpenAIのAIエージェントが無断で侵入していたと発表した。注目すべ
きは、これが悪意あるハッカーによるサイバー攻撃ではなく、OpenAIの研究チームがAIに調査を
実行させる過程で起きた点だ。AIは調査中にアクセス制限に直面すると、自ら制限を回避する方
法を見つけ出し、非公開のファイルにまでアクセスしたという。この事例は、AIが与えられた目
的を達成する過程で、許可された範囲を超えてシステムにアクセスしうることを示した。 
サイバー攻撃の脅威は、海の向こうだけの話ではない。日本でも9月下旬以降、物流、交通、
小売、カーシェアリングなど幅広い業種の企業が、不正アクセスによる個人情報流出の可能性を
相次いで公表している。これらの攻撃にAIが用いられたかどうかは明らかになっていないが、サ
イバー攻撃の影響が業種を問わず及んでいることは確かだ。政府も10月1 日から、能動的サイバ

### Page 2

2/6 
ー防御の一環として、重大なサイバー攻撃を未然に防ぐため、警察や自衛隊が攻撃元のサーバー
等にアクセスし、無害化措置を講じることを可能にした。 
AIがサイバー攻撃を自動化・高度化させる時代が到来しつつある。企業や政府の防御需要は急
増し、サイバーセキュリティ企業には特需がもたらされると期待される。個人投資家の間の「AI
の次なる恩恵銘柄はサイバー株」という見方にも、一定の説得力がある。しかし、 「サイバー株な
ら何でも上がる」と考えるのは早計である。AIによる攻撃の自動化・高度化は、攻撃件数を増や
すだけでなく、従来のセキュリティ製品や防御手法の有効性そのものを揺るがしかねないから
だ。本稿では、AIの攻撃能力の現在地と、それがサイバーセキュリティ企業の業績および株価に
与える影響を、データに基づいて検証する。そのうえで、 「サイバー株＝安全確実な成長株」とい
う見方をどう見直すべきか、個人投資家に向けて投資戦略を考えるための新たな視点を提示した
い。

2. AIの攻撃能力急伸とサイバーセキュリティ企業の業績・株価への影響 
(1) AIは人間なしでどこまで攻撃できるのか 
AIエージェントのサイバー攻撃関連能力は急速に高まっている。スタンフォード大学人間中心
AI研究所（Stanford HAI）が公表した「AI Index Report 2026」によれば、AIは人間が逐次指示
を与えなくても、専門的なサイバー課題を自律的に解ける水準に達しつつある。 
同レポートで紹介されている「Cybench」は、暗号、Webセキュリティ、リバースエンジニアリ
ング、フォレンジック、エクスプロイトなど、40の専門レベルのサイバーセキュリティ課題を評
価するベンチマークである。資料1 は、人間のガイドなし（unguided）でAIが課題を解決できた
割合の推移を示している。これを見ると、2024年時点ではわずか15％であった解決率が、2025年
には 82％へと急伸し、2026年には93％に達している。2年間で 78ポイントの上昇であり、AIが
自律的にサイバー課題を解く能力が大きく向上していることがわかる。

### Page 3

3/6 
資料 1 AI エージェントの自律的なサイバー課題解決能力は急伸している 
（注）サイバーセキュリティのベンチマーク「Cybench」における、人間のガイドなし（unguided）での課題解決率（％）の推移。
Cybench は暗号、Web セキュリティ、リバースエンジニアリング、フォレンジック、エクスプロイトなど 40 の専門レベル課題を評価
するもの。 
（出所）Stanford HAI, “AI Index Report 2026”をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

(2) サイバー需要は企業の売上・利益に届いているのか 
こうしたAIによるサイバー攻撃の脅威が高まるなか、防御側であるサイバーセキュリティ企業
への需要拡大が期待される。実際、その需要はすでに企業業績に表れている。 
Nasdaqが公表した「Q1 2026 HXRXL Cybersecurity Commentary」によれば、サイバーセキュリ
ティ関連銘柄で構成される「Nasdaq ISE Cyber Security Select Index（HXRXL） 」構成企業の
2026 年第1 四半期決算は極めて好調であった。同レポートによると、構成企業の売上高は前年同
期比で 11％増加し、純利益は同29.3％の増加を記録しており、売上高以上に純利益が大きく伸び
ていることが確認できる。さらに、市場予想に対する結果をまとめた資料2を見ても、売上高で
予想を上回った企業は指数ウェイトの92.2％を占め、純利益に至っては100％の企業が予想を上
回った。サイバーセキュリティ需要の強さは、売上高と利益の双方に表れている。

### Page 4

4/6 
資料 2 サイバーセキュリティ企業の業績は市場予想を大きく上回る

高および純利益の予想超え・予想未達の企業数（指数ウェイトベース）、ならびに平均上振れ・下振れ率（％）を示す。 
（出所）Nasdaq, “Q1 2026 HXRXL Cybersecurity Commentary”をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

(3) それでもサイバー株は上がるのか 
業績が好調であれば株価も上昇すると考えるのが自然だが、 「AIによる攻撃高度化＝サイバー株
上昇」とは必ずしも言い切れない。AIは防御需要を増やす一方で、既存のサイバーセキュリティ
製品を陳腐化させるとの懸念も生んでいるからだ。 
国際決済銀行（BIS）が2026 年7月に公表したレポート「A Mythos moment? Frontier AI and 
cyber risk」は、このパラドックスを指摘している。2026年 4月にAnthropicが高度なサイバー攻
撃能力を持つAIモデル「Mythos」を発表した際、サイバーセキュリティ関連株は大きく下落し
た。資料3 は、Mythos発表前を0とした場合のサイバーセキュリティ関連株の累積異常リターン
（CAR）を示している。発表後、サイバーセキュリティETF（IHAK）や主要企業（CRWD、PANWな
ど）のCARは軒並みマイナスに転じ、一部の系列ではグラフ上で約−30％の大幅なマイナスを記録
した。BISは、この市場反応について「新しいAIモデルの登場により、既存のサイバーセキュリテ
ィ製品やサービスが陳腐化する（obsolescence）という投資家の懸念を反映している」との解釈
を紹介している。つまり、AIの進化はサイバーセキュリティ市場全体を拡大させる可能性が高い
ものの、同時にゲームチェンジを引き起こし、旧来の技術やサービスに依存する企業の競争力を
急速に奪うリスクを孕んでいる。

3. AI時代のサイバー株投資～「陳腐化リスク」を回避する新戦略～ 
第 2 章で見てきたように、AI はサイバー攻撃能力を飛躍的に高めている。また、足元のサイバ
ーセキュリティ企業の業績も好調である。しかし、最先端のAIモデルが登場した際には、市場は
既存のサイバーセキュリティ製品の「陳腐化リスク」に敏感に反応し、関連株は大きく売り込ま
れた。ここからいえるのは、サイバーセキュリティ市場全体は今後も拡大が見込まれるものの、
企業間の「勝ち負け」はこれまで以上に鮮明になるということだ。こうした環境変化のなか、個
人投資家には従来の投資アプローチを根本から見直すことが求められる。 
第一に、サイバーセキュリティセクター全体を丸ごと買うアプローチ（テーマ型ETFやインデ
ックスファンドなど）には注意が必要である。市場全体が成長しても、AIの進化に取り残され、
陳腐化して淘汰される旧世代の企業をポートフォリオに抱え込むリスクが高まるからだ。足元の
業績や過去の市場シェアだけで安心することはできない。第二に、個別銘柄を選別する際は、 「AI
ネイティブ」なサイバーセキュリティ企業であるかどうかが最大の焦点となる。攻撃側がAI を駆
使する以上、防御側もAIをシステムの中心に据え、未知の脅威を自律的に検知・遮断できるプラ
ットフォームを持っていなければ対抗できない。レガシーな製品を販売している企業ではなく、
クラウドベースで膨大なデータをAI で解析し、防御機能を継続的に高度化できる企業を見極める
必要がある。第三に、サイバー防衛の投資対象を「セキュリティ専業企業」以外にも広げること
である。 
最先端のAI モデルを開発し、強固なクラウドインフラを持つ巨大テック企業（メガテック）
は、自社のAI を活用した防衛技術を、顧客向けの強力なセキュリティサービスとして提供し始め

経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。

Document 363: Quantum dictionary, part 1_ Decoding the fundamentals From superposition to decoherence, we unpack the concepts and technologies shaping the future of quantum computing. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Quantum dictionary, part 1_ Decoding the fundamentals From superposition to decoherence, we unpack the concepts and technologies shaping the future of quantum computing. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 9

### Page 1

13030420     1

Transformation 
Quantum dictionary, part 1: Decoding the fundamentals 
21 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• What is quantum computing? How does it work? What is a qubit? And when will quantum reach its full potential? Quantum 
computing challenges our intuition and our understanding of what computers can do. In this two-part dictionary series, we 
decode key terms behind what could be the next major technological revolution. 
• Qubits are the basic building blocks of quantum computers, harnessing properties such as superposition and entanglement to 
process information. However, qubits are highly sensitive to their surroundings where disturbances can lead to errors in 
quantum computation, making error correction a key challenge for building quantum computers. 
• There is no single approach to building a quantum computer. Researchers and companies are pursuing a range of qubit 
technologies, from neutral atoms and photons to spin, superconducting, topological and trapped-ion qubits, while quantum 
computing itself is likely to increasingly work alongside classical computing and AI.

First, let’s lay the groundwork… 
Quantum computing  
Quantum computing uses qubits and quantum properties such as superposition (defined later on) to perform complex 
calculations and solve certain problems faster than classical computers.1 From a practical perspective, quantum computing could 
be used to tackle problems related to optimization, machine learning, simulation and factorization across a range of sectors, 
including autos and logistics, financial services, energy and climate, health and life sciences and national security. 
Current quantum computers can perform ‘just’ thousands of quantum operations. As the technology advances from thousands to 
millions, billions and eventually trillions of operations, quantum computers could begin to have commercial applications.  
• Millions: Financial services and security could experience some of the first use cases, such as threat detection, fraud 
prevention, derivatives pricing, bond trading and arbitrage. 
• Billions: Quantum could help power AI through advanced data generation capabilities.  
• Trillions: Quantum chemistry applications could emerge, including drug discovery, battery simulation and materials 
discovery. 
These are the publicly known applications, according to Oxford Quantum Circuits (OQC). And as quantum computing capabilities 
improve, some of these use cases could emerge sooner than anticipated.  
Now, we’ll tackle the core concepts… 
Qubits 
Qubits (quantum bits) are a quantum computer’s basic unit of information. They can exhibit quantum properties such as 
superposition and entanglement (which we elaborate on below) — properties fundamental to quantum computing. 
Quantum gates manipulate the state of qubits, much like logic gates manipulate bits in classical computers. Despite the name, a 
quantum gate is not a physical object, but rather an operation applied to a quantum system that changes its state. 
From physical to logical qubits 
Physical qubits are the foundation of a quantum computer’s hardware. However, they are highly susceptible to error. To address 
this, multiple physical qubits can be structured together to encode a logical qubit, allowing errors to be detected and corrected

1 Schneider, J., & Smalley, I. (2025, June 10). What is quantum computing? IBM. 
I NSTI TUTE
Accessible version

### Page 2

2 21 October 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

and making the stored quantum information more reliable. In recent years, according to BofA Global Research, logical qubits 
have become a greater focus for quantum computing companies. 
Entanglement 
Entanglement is a condition where two or more qubits become linked so that their states are correlated, regardless of distance 
(Exhibit 1). It allows quantum computers to process information in ways that can be difficult or impossible for classical 
computers. 
So, how does it work? Suppose we have two photons that have opposite polarizations (the orientation of their light waves). 
Before measurement, neither has a definite polarization. When one is measured as horizontally polarized, the other will be 
vertically polarized when measured in the same basis, wherever it is. When measured separately, they appear to be random, but 
when compared, they are correlated (Exhibit 2). 
The challenges 
Once created, entanglement must be protected from decoherence (where interactions with the environment destroy quantum 
correlations). Maintaining entanglement over long distances is another challenge. Photons traveling through optical fiber can be 
absorbed or scattered, making long-distance quantum communication difficult. 2 Quantum repeaters aim to overcome this 
challenge by extending entanglement across a series of shorter links. 
Exhibit 1: Entanglement is a state where at least two qubits become 
correlated 
An infographic of entangled qubits

BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Exhibit 2: Measuring one qubit in an entangled pair instantly 
determines the state of the other, no matter the distance 
An example of what entanglement means for qubit measurement

Source: BofA Global Research 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Superposition 
Unlike a classical bit, which can be one of two states (0 or 1), superposition allows a quantum system to exist in multiple states 
or configurations at the same time (Exhibit 3). For example, a qubit can exist in the 0 state, the 1 state or any state that is a 
combination of 0 and 1.3  
A quantum system can maintain a superposition until it is observed or measured. Once measured, its superposition collapses into 
a single state. Another way of thinking about superposition is to imagine a sphere with the north pole representing zero and the 
south pole representing one. Other points on the surface of the sphere represent different superposition states. This allows us 
to: 1) represent complex forms of information; and 2) simulate atoms in a computer. 
Schrödinger’s cat 
In 1935, physicist Erwin Schrödinger proposed a thought experiment to illustrate the strange implications of quantum 
mechanics. In this scenario, a cat, a radioactive substance that decays very slowly, and a Geiger counter are put together in a 
sealed box. According to quantum mechanics, the system can be described as a superposition in which the cat is both alive and 
dead until the box is opened. Once the box is opened and the system is observed, the superposition collapses into a single 
outcome.

2 Rehman, M. (2026, May 11). What is Quantum Entanglement? Quantum Insider. 
3 Microsoft. (n.d.). Superposition.

I NSTI TUTE

Enabling parallel computation versus classical computing 
Superposition is a key attribute of quantum computing that allows for quantum parallelism, or the ability to perform multiple 
computations at the same time. Classical bits can only be in one of two states, 0 or 1. In contrast, a qubit can be in a 
superposition of 0 and 1, allowing a quantum computer to process multiple possible qubit states at once.4  
Exhibit 3: Superposition is the condition where a qubit can exist in a superposition of 0 and 1 
Illustrating the power of superposition

Source: BofA Global Research 
Note: A Bloch sphere is a geometric representation of qubit states. 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Error rates and fidelity 
Fidelity is a measure of how closely a quantum state or operation matches what was intended (Exhibit 4). A higher fidelity 
generally means fewer errors. 
Physical qubits are prone to errors. One way to address this is to combine multiple physical qubits to create a more reliable 
logical qubit. Quantum error correction (QEC) allows errors in the physical qubits to be detected and corrected, helping protect 
the information stored in the logical qubit.

4 Ibid.

### Page 4

4 21 October 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

Exhibit 4: Fidelity measures how accurately a qubit retains its intended quantum state during computation 
Infographic showing the implications of high versus low fidelity

Source: BofA Global Research  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
How does quantum error correction work? 
At its simplest, QEC relies on redundancy, or storing information across multiple qubits so that errors can be detected and 
corrected.  
Consider an example: suppose someone wants to send a message represented by 0. Instead of sending a single 0, the message 
could be repeated three times as 000, known as a codeword. If one bit were accidentally flipped from 0 to 1 during transmission, 
the recipient might receive 010. Because two of the three bits are still 0, the recipient could infer that the intended message 
was 000. 
However, if two bits flipped, producing 110 or 011, the recipient could infer the wrong message. If all three flipped to 111, the 
recipient would have no way to know from the received codeword that errors had occurred.5 This approach is known as a 
repetition code and illustrates how redundancy can help protect information from errors.  
Repetition codes are a simple form of error correction that can address bit-flip errors, where a 0 changes to a 1 or vice versa. 
More sophisticated QEC codes are needed to address other quantum errors, such as phase flips.6 Shor’s code, developed by 
American theoretical computer scientist Peter Shor, uses nine physical qubits to encode one logical qubit and can correct one 
bit-flip error or one phase flip error, but it can’t correct both types of errors at the same time. Meanwhile, Steane’s seven-qubit 
code, developed by Oxford physicist Andrew Steane, can also correct both types of errors. It has the advantage of being fault-
tolerant, meaning the error-correction process itself does not introduce additional errors.7 
At what error level might we start seeing quantum advantage? 
Some classical computing systems today operate with extremely high reliability, often described as having "17 nines" of fidelity 
(99.99999999999999999%). By comparison, quantum computers currently achieve fidelity rates of around 99.9%. Gerald 
Mullally, CEO of OQC, has argued that quantum systems would need to add roughly three more nines of fidelity to achieve 
commercial relevance, or quantum advantage. 
Noise and decoherence 
Noise refers to unwanted disturbances that affect quantum systems and can lead to errors in computations (Exhibit 5). It can 
come from sources such as interactions with the environment, electromagnetic interference and temperature fluctuations. 
Coherence is a quantum system’s ability to maintain its quantum properties, including superposition and entanglement. When 
coherence is lost through interactions with the environment, the process is known as “decoherence,” causing the system to 
behave more classically (Exhibit 6).8

5 Microsoft Ignite. (n.d.) Introduction to quantum error correction. 
6 Ibid. 
7 Ibid. 
8 Quandela. (n.d.) Quantum Decoherence.

### Page 5

21 October 2026    5

I NSTI TUTE

To fully control qubits for calculations and information transfer, they need to be carefully protected from their surroundings. 
External factors like noise, energy, changes in Earth’s magnetic field, radiation from Wi-Fi or mobile phones or cosmic rays can 
interfere with the process. Even small disturbances can lead to decoherence, making it difficult to develop large-scale, fault-
tolerant quantum computers.9 
According to QuEra Computing, quantum noise can be mitigated through physical isolation of qubits, the development of more 
precise control techniques and quantum error correction, which helps detect and correct errors in physical qubits.  
Exhibit 5: Noise refers to unwanted disturbances, e.g., interactions 
with the environment, that lead to errors in quantum computation

BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Quantum processing unit (QPU) 
Referred to as the brain of the quantum computer, the QPU is the core hardware where qubits reside and where quantum 
computations take place. QPUs rely on quantum behaviors like superposition and entanglement to tackle problems in areas such 
as cryptography, simulation and optimization. Just as a CPU (central processing unit) in classical computing contains multiple 
components, a QPU includes quantum gates (operations that manipulate qubits in a controlled way, similar to logic gates in 
classical computing) and interconnects, which enable communication between qubits and other quantum processors.10 
Next, we’ll look at broader computing architecture… 
Heterogeneous computing  
Heterogeneous computing refers to a future computing landscape likely combining classical and quantum computing, potentially 
unlocking more powerful, faster and cheaper compute and helping solve problems that classical computers cannot. According to 
BofA Global Research, AI and quantum computing could also converge and complement each other, enabling more 
transformative use cases. 
Quantum modality  
A quantum modality refers to the type of physical technology used to create and manipulate qubits. We discuss the modalities in 
greater depth in the following section. 
Lastly, we outline qubit technologies… 
Neutral atom qubit 
A quantum modality that uses individual atoms with no electrical charge as qubits (Exhibit 7). The atoms are held in place using 
focused lasers known as optical tweezers. Neutral atom qubit systems do not require atoms to be ionized, which means fewer 
energy constraints and potentially simpler cooling requirements. Atoms such as rubidium or cesium are typically used. The qubits 
are manipulated by changing the energy levels or positions of the trapped atoms using laser pulses.

9 QuEra. (n.d.) Quantum Noise. 
10 SpinQ. (2025, January 16). What does a Quantum Computer Look Like? Explain Key Parts.

  I NSTI TUTE

Exhibit 7: A quantum modality where atoms with a net electrical charge of zero are the foundation for qubits 
Key features of neutral atoms

Source: BofA Global Research 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Photonic qubit 
A quantum modality that uses particles of light, known as photons, to carry quantum information (Exhibit 8).11 Optical 
components such as beam splitters, which divide light along different paths, and phase shifters, which change the phase of light, 
can be used to perform quantum operations. Photonic quantum computers can use integrated photonic chips to manipulate 
photons and enable quantum logic. 
Exhibit 8: A quantum modality relying on the quantum properties of light 
Key features of photonic qubits

Source: BofA Global Research 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

11 Moore, J. (2025, March 28). The 6 different types of quantum computing technology. TechTarget.

### Page 7

21 October 2026    7

I NSTI TUTE

Spin qubit 
A quantum modality that uses the intrinsic spin of electrons or nuclei in solid-state materials, such as silicon or diamond. These 
spins can be manipulated using magnetic or electric fields, and their states are read using optical or charge-sensing techniques. 
Spin qubits are particularly appealing because they can leverage existing CMOS (complementary metal-oxide semiconductor; a 
technology to build processors) manufacturing infrastructure, making it easier to integrate them with conventional processors. 
Superconducting qubit 
A quantum modality in which electronic circuits create qubits. They are built using Josephson junctions, where two 
superconductors are separated by a thin insulating layer. Microwave pulses manipulate the qubits’ quantum states to run 
computations.12  
Specifically, a resistance-free current oscillates back and forth around a quantum loop. An injected microwave signal excites the 
current into superposition states.13 To enable this process, superconducting materials are cooled to near absolute zero (around 
10-20 millikelvin) using dilution refrigerators, helping reduce thermal noise and maintain coherence. 
Topological qubit 
A quantum modality that aims to encode quantum information using topological properties rather than relying only on individual 
particle states (Exhibit 9). One way to think about this is through the paths certain quasiparticles take as they move around one 
another, creating “braids” in space-time. Because the information can depend on the overall pattern of these braids rather than 
small details of their paths, the approach is designed to be more resilient to certain disturbances. 
Exhibit 9: Resilient to small perturbations but requires special quasiparticles not yet been conclusively demonstrated in nature 
An infographic of topological qubits

Source: BofA Global Research 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Trapped-ion qubit  
A quantum modality in which ions (electrically charged atoms) are held in place using electromagnetic fields. These ions act as 
the qubits and are controlled by the system with tightly focused laser beams.14 The qubits are held inside ultra-high vacuum 
(UHV) chambers.

An update on AI and US equities

Both stocks and investor views relating to the AI trade have been exceptionally 
volatile during the last few months. Most of the volatility has been concentrated in the 
AI infrastructure complex, but the latest earnings season also drove sharp moves in the 
hyperscalers and among software stocks. 
Below we address three of the most common AI-related questions we have received 
from clients in recent weeks: 
1. What is the outlook for AI capex? 
2. What revenues are required to generate a sufficient return on recent AI 
investments? 
3. What outlook for AI is the equity market pricing?

1. What is the outlook for AI capex? 
The largest US hyperscalers are on track to spend $800 billion in capex in 2026 and 
consensus estimates imply they will spend $1.1 trillion in 2027. Consensus 2027 
capex estimates have been revised higher by $175 billion since the start of the Q2 
earnings season. Commentary from the hyperscalers during earnings season described a 
supply-constrained environment and large revenue backlogs, and these factors are 
driving the companies to tap both debt and equity markets to support additional 
investment spending. Based on consensus estimates, capex in 2027 is on track to reach a

larger share of GDP than any technological investment cycle since the railroad build-out

Our baseline forecast is that hyperscaler capex spending will increase substantially 
in 2027 but that the rate of both capex growth and upside surprises to consensus 
estimates will diminish relative to recent quarters. GS equity analysts estimate that 
hyperscaler capex will register $1.2 trillion in 2027 and $1.4 trillion in 2028. Their 
estimates are $130 billion and $100 billion above the consensus estimates, respectively, 
but nonetheless imply a deceleration in capex growth from nearly 100% in 2026 to 54% 
in 2027 and 12% in 2028.

The need for external financing and potential supply bottlenecks will likely 
constrain the rate of capex growth next year. Hyperscaler capex spending now 
exceeds cash flow from operations, which means large further growth will increasingly 
need funding via debt and equity issuance. While hyperscalers have added leverage, 
their balance sheets do not appear to be a binding constraint and their interest coverage 
ratios remain extremely healthy. Our credit strategists instead point to concentration in 
the investment grade market as the more relevant bottleneck, which will require 
additional funding from private credit and equity issuance. Potential government 
restrictions on data center construction or an intentional slowdown in frontier model 
roll-out could also act as potential constraints on spending, as could shortages of labor, 
power, memory, or other inputs. Constraints on capex represent a risk to the near-term 
earnings of AI infrastructure beneficiaries, but could help ease investor concerns about 
overbuilding.

Going forward, token demand growth will need to outpace declining token prices to 
support continued growth in investment spending. Frontier models are currently a 
key source of demand for hyperscaler compute. However, the rise of competitive 
open-source models has contributed to a decline in average token prices. Measures of 
frontier token demand slowed in July, simultaneous with sell-offs of many AI 
infrastructure stocks, but accelerated again in August. 
For most AI infrastructure companies, decelerating AI investment spending 
suggests decelerating EPS growth. AI infrastructure companies, and semis in 
particular, have been the clearest beneficiaries of the capex surge. Continued upward 
revisions to consensus 2027 capex estimates should support continued positive 
revisions to consensus 2027 EPS estimates for many AI infrastructure stocks, but the 
magnitude of revisions will likely diminish relative to what occurred in H1 2026.

### Page 5

2. What revenues are required to generate a return on AI investments? 
Our equity analysts estimate that the hyperscalers would need to generate annual 
AI revenues of roughly $300 billion in the next few years to break even on their 
average annual capex investments in 2026 and 2027. These estimates reflect their 
expectations for average annual AI capex in 2026 and 2027 for the US hyperscalers, 
including AMZN, GOOGL, META, MSFT, ORCL, and SPCX. The breakeven revenues 
required for this capex represent the sum of annual depreciation and operating 
expenses. Exhibit 8 provides an estimated sensitivity of required AI revenues the 
hyperscalers must generate in different return on invested capital (“ROIC”) scenarios. 
These required revenue estimates are anchored on average annual capex in 2026 and 
2027 and would argue for even larger estimates based on cumulative capex.

### Page 6

The hyperscalers’ current estimated AI revenues remain below the amount that 
would allow them to break even on their capex, but those revenues are growing 
quickly and revenue backlogs are sizable. The hyperscalers have started to monetize 
their AI investments. Year/year cloud revenue growth for AMZN, GOOGL, MSFT, and 
ORCL has accelerated from 25% in 2024 to 48% in Q2 2026. In dollar terms, annualized 
cloud revenues in excess of their 2024 growth rate registered nearly $70 billion in Q2 
and have been roughly doubling during the past few quarters. The three major US public 
cloud companies (AMZN, GOOGL, MSFT) have also announced a combined revenue 
backlog of $1.7 trillion, which provides line of sight into these companies’ ability to meet 
required revenue targets.

### Page 7

The magnitude of capex in 2026 and 2027 points to even larger “required” annual 
revenues in the next few years for the “application layer” of companies buying 
much of that compute from the hyperscalers. These revenues reflect the additional 
costs and profit margins of application companies on top of the “required” hyperscaler 
revenues selling compute. EBIT margins for large-cap software companies have 
averaged 40% during the past five years. We utilize EBIT margins rather than gross 
margins to include the costs of both training and inference, and assume additional 
expenses alongside compute. The range of potential required revenues is wide 
depending on which companies constitute the application layer and how their costs are 
structured.  
Exhibit 11 provides a range of potential “required” revenues based on hyperscaler 
ROIC and application layer EBIT margins. For example, the application layer would 
have to generate over $1 trillion in annual revenues if those companies had a 30% EBIT 
margin and the hyperscalers generated a 10%-20% return on invested capital. 
The “required” application layer revenues that would justify current capex are large 
but potentially achievable. For example, estimates of global advertising and software 
spending each equal roughly $1.5 trillion in 2026. Our economists estimate that total 
annual AI US labor productivity gains that will potentially accrue to capital total roughly 
$1.8 trillion. However, not all of these gains will necessarily accrue to the infrastructure 
and application layer companies. In addition, another year of strong capex growth in 
2028 would raise the hurdle further. AI-related revenues and capex needs would be 
substantially larger accounting for physical AI or the potential emergence of AGI.

### Page 8

3. What outlook for AI is the equity market pricing? 
While market participants express skepticism about the durability of AI 
infrastructure stocks’ earnings growth, that view is at least partially priced. The 
median AI infrastructure stock has de-rated from 32x consensus forward earnings in 
April 2026 to 22x today. In addition to tracking standard P/E multiples, comparing 
recent changes in market cap to recent changes in earnings can signal how long the 
market expects current earnings strength to persist. Among various AI infrastructure 
baskets, that ratio remains most elevated among optical networking, electrical grid, and 
liquid cooling stocks while expectations appear most conservative among 
semiconductors and utilities. 
Semiconductor valuations appear to embed particularly skeptical views regarding 
the sustainability of current earnings strength. Valuations have declined sharply 
among Semis stocks, which is typical among cyclical companies with strong near-term 
growth but uncertain medium-term growth. We believe the investment opportunity is 
more compelling in fabless chip stocks than in memory stocks. Our equity analysts are 
Buy-rated on NVDA and AVGO but Neutral-rated on MU.

### Page 9

Memory stock prices imply earnings will decline in coming years, but the degree of 
pessimism does not appear particularly extreme relative to past cycles. Memory 
stocks currently trade at a 2-year forward P/E of 4x, half their 15-year average, but 
Memory firms currently enjoy gross margins of roughly 80%, more than double their 
historical average. To bring the current P/E of memory stocks to its 15-year average, 
earnings in coming years would need to decline by roughly 50%. While this decline is 
large in absolute terms, memory is a cyclical industry and has experienced larger EPS 
declines, including outright negative earnings, during previous cycles. This pricing will 
prove too pessimistic if 2028 forecasts are realized, but many investors have expressed 
skepticism about rotating back into higher beta pockets of the infrastructure trade as 
capex growth begins to normalize.

### Page 10

In contrast, the fabless chip stocks also trade at discounted valuations but have had 
less extreme margin expansion. Margins for the largest fabless chip stocks are only 
modestly above their historical average. Earnings in the next few years would need to 
decline by about 10% to bring the P/E back to its historical average. This is similar to the 
magnitude of historical realized cyclical earnings declines for the group.

The likelihood of decelerating capex growth should be a positive catalyst for the 
hyperscalers. We previously laid out three conditions for the hyperscalers to regain 
leadership. These include decelerating macroeconomic growth, demonstrated 
monetization of AI investment, and decelerating capex growth. Our economists believe 
US economic growth is no longer accelerating. The hyperscalers have already started to 
demonstrate signs of monetization, and we expect capex growth will slow in 2027. 
Uncertainty about the length, magnitude, and return on investment for this capex cycle 
will likely persist. However, the hyperscalers have de-rated to their lowest P/E multiples 
in over a decade, and the group now trades just four turns above the median S&P 500 
company, its smallest premium on record. While investors have recently been focused 
more on the hyperscalers’ negative free cash flows than on their earnings, visibility into 
improving FCF as revenues accelerate and capex decelerates should be supportive for 
the stocks.

### Page 11

We expect the progression of the AI trade towards application roll-out will remain a 
source of return dispersion. We believe the “winners” and “losers” from the application 
layer will take several quarters to play out, as AI applications get built and adoption 
broadens. 
The threat of disruption had weighed on the share prices of many Software stocks 
earlier this year, but the group has rebounded sharply in recent weeks. Reported 
Software fundamentals have shown little sign of AI disruption, with annual recurring 
revenue growth for the median stock in GS analyst coverage accelerating slightly in 
recent quarters. A group of Software stocks has rallied by 45% and is now within 8% of 
its high reached in late 2025. Software stocks focused on IT infrastructure and 
cybersecurity have posted the strongest returns, while enterprise management software 
remain below their previous highs.

### Page 12

Consumer-facing businesses with high switching costs have also recently emerged 
as a potential area of disruption. The increasing use of agents, including by new 
consumer-facing tools such as Muse, may disrupt the business models of some 
consumer firms. Our colleagues in Global Banking & Markets developed a basket of 
“consumer inertia” stocks (GSXUSWCH) where user stickiness is driven more by effort 
required to take action rather than product differentiation. The basket includes 
insurance, telecom, travel, subscriptions, marketplaces, and fintech. The basket has 
lagged a sector-neutral hedge by 5 pp since mid-September.

### Page 13

Enterprise AI adoption remains in early stages but spending has recently 
accelerated, suggesting the impact of AI on corporate earnings should grow 
increasingly visible in coming quarters. The Census Bureau survey suggests that 40% 
of large US firms are using AI in the production of goods and services. Our analysis of Q2 
earnings commentary suggests roughly 50% of S&P 500 companies are using AI in the 
context of productivity, but very few companies have quantified the productivity uplift 
today. However, the revenue run rates of model providers and alternative measures of 
company spending on AI have accelerated during the last several months alongside the 
increasing use of agentic harnesses. 
In contrast with past periods of market exuberance, the equity market today does 
not appear to be pricing expectations of widespread productivity gains. Baskets of 
AI productivity beneficiaries have traded roughly sideways on a relative basis YTD, 
matching the trajectory of near-term forward EPS estimates. Similarly, the measure of 
market-implied long-term growth expectations in Exhibit 22 is only slightly above the 
historical average (for details, see US Equity Views: The next phases of the AI trade).

### Page 1

1
EXPERT TAKE FOLLOW THE MONEY
What’s the Renminbi Got to Do With it?
A closer look at the impact of China’s currency on China’s export growth.
Sean Yong/Reuters
UPDATED
October 11, 2026 12:00 a.m.
Articles Follow the Money Share
Log In
Regions Topics Research Experts Events The Future of American Strategy

### Page 2

2
Brad W. Setser CFR EXPERT
Whitney Shepardson Senior Fellow
There is an argument—made in the recent Geneva
Report and by various former IMF officials—that
China’s current export surge is the simple
consequence of the housing downturn and the
resulting weakness in Chinese internal demand.*
The weakness in the RMB in this story is entirely
incidental to the increase in exports, which is, more or
less, a mechanical response to weak internal demand.

Cannot sell excavators at home? Export them.
Built more modern car capacity than your internal
market supports? Export that too…
The fall in the nominal RMB over the course of 2022
and the first half of 2023 in nominal and read terms
was simply a function of domestic macroeconomic
weakness, and a growing rate differential. It wasn’t an
active policy choice by China’s central bank.

### Page 3

3
Now, some might note that China’s currency doesn’t
always move with rate differentials. The RMB didn’t
consistently get pulled up when Chinese rates were
higher than U.S. rates (2002-2020 generally
speaking), and while it did fall when rate differentials
moved against the RMB in 2022 and 2023, it then
stabilized and has even appreciated modestly since
the middle of last year. That appreciation hasn’t been
big enough to correct the deep undervaluation of the

RMB, but it came at a time when the PBOC policy
rate (the reverse repo rate) was well below the U.S.
policy rate.
China doesn’t have an open financial account. As a
result, equilibrium in the foreign exchange market is
thus driven far more by exporter conversion and
current account flows than by rate differentials and
financial flows.** That, together with China’s ongoing
management of the exchange rate and a trade surplus
that can generate significant inflows, means that the

### Page 4

4
value of the RMB is much more of a policy variable
under the independent control of the Chinese
authorities than a simple byproduct of monetary and
fiscal policies.
The modern IMF is reluctant to accept this
conclusion. Its surveillance has generally ignored
China’s currency management (even if that
management is relatively obvious; see the data on FX
settlement) and its recent comprehensive assessment
of China’s economy completely ignored the balance of

payments and exchange rate management.
But it is pretty obvious to the market, even if the
PBOC, rather absurdly, insists that “market forces
play a decisive role in determining the RMB
exchange rate.”
I doubt the PBOC can win this argument. I agree
with Mark Sobel:
“The renminbi is still heavily managed and the
authorities have acted to slow appreciation. The paper
states that the PBoC phased out ‘regular’ foreign
exchange intervention in 2017. But it has many

means at its disposal to achieve the same effect
through extensive capital controls, administrative
guidance, signalling at the fix and deploying state-
owned commercial banks. These means are heavily
used. Thus, the word ‘regular’ must be taken with a
pinch of salt. The comparison to Japan, which

### Page 5

5
intervenes occasionally, is spurious – the yen largely
floats freely in integrated global markets. The same
cannot be said for the renminbi.”
In reality, the PBOC’s daily fix now plays the decisive
role in the currency’s path, and the market is often a
bet on how the PBOC’s management of the yuan will
evolve.
That is clear from the lack of volatility in the yuan’s
rate against the dollar. The yuan’s move down against
the dollar in 2022 was a controlled 10 percent move,

and the appreciation over the last 16 months has been
even more controlled crawl.
And the market is clearly massaged with substantial
help from the state banks. In fact that the pattern of
changes in the monthly FX settlement data looks
exactly like the pattern of intervention of a central
bank managing its currency around a tight band (the

### Page 6

6
state banks in practice buy FX to keep the CNY from
testing the strong side of the band, so there is an
asymmetry in how the CNY is managed)
None of this would matter much if China’s economy
was small. But that obviously isn’t the case. Nor would
this matter much if China’s exports didn’t respond to
changes in the RMB’s real (and nominal) value. But
that is also not the case.
There is abundant evidence that China’s exports do
respond to moves in the currency. Periods of RMB

weakness have consistently been associated with
periods of especially rapid Chinese export growth.

### Page 7

7
Chancellor Merz and President Macron got this
right, even if macroeconomic issues haven’t yet
figured prominently in the EU’s dialogue with
China.*** As they note in their open letter to the
European Commission:
“An appreciation of undervalued currencies towards a value
more in line with fundamentals would help support the
rebalancing of trade accounts with the EU in the short and
medium term … European policy discussions focused on
forging a common understanding of these exchange rate issues

and their interaction with global imbalances would be useful.”
The best evidence that exports responded to changes
in the currency, and not simply to changes in the path
of China’s internal demand, is the acceleration in
China’s export growth after the RMB’s depreciation
over the course of 2022.
This is the killer chart.

### Page 8

8
Now, you might ask: what of the acceleration in
export growth in 2020 and 2021? That was pretty
clearly tied to the global increase in goods demand
that came with the pandemic and work from home
requirements. It was expected to fade when the world
economy normalized. But it didn’t. By 2023 Chinese
export growth again topped global trade growth.
The general expectation that the rise in China’s goods
surplus during Covid would prove to be “transitory”

### Page 9

9
heavily influenced the IMF’s work at the time: see the
IMF’s external sector reports of 2023 and 2024.
I would argue that neglecting the exchange rate was
the key reason the IMF’s kept forecasting, incorrectly,
that China’s current account surplus would fall (See
this blog with Shahin Vallee). The IMF certainly
didn’t warn that China’s property downturn and the
RMB’s real depreciation risked a second China shock
in its 2022, 2023, or 2024 surveillance reports.

Concretely, the weaker RMB appears to have
contributed to a very large outperformance of China’s
exports relative to global trade. See the following
chart
That incidentally also happened after the RMB
followed the dollar down in 2003.
In both periods, there were significant structural
shifts. The WTO accession in 2002, and the success

### Page 10

10
of Chinese industrial policies in the “green” industrial
sectors and autos in 2022. In both cses exports were
turbocharged when structural shifts that should have
resulted in a stronger currency were instead amplified
by currency weakness.
No matter. Paul Krugman has noted that a country
that is converging technologically with its trade
partners should see a trend appreciation in its
currency. China got the technological convergence,
but not the real appreciation—at least not until this

year. And the move since the middle of 2025 has
corrected the depreciation of the RMB that occurred
when the RMB followed the dollar down on
liberation day, but not the structural overvaluation.
Now, there is an argument that exchange rate
adjustment is too hot an issue to make it a priority.
The PBOC isn’t interested it seems.
And some believe that in the interest of comity and
good relations with China, the IMF should mirror
the PBOC’s views. After all a stronger RMB would

slow China’s exports and thus slow China’s economy
at a time when China needs export growth to escape
deflation and generate employment. Or so the
argument goes. Concerns about “external balance“
and the negative global spillovers from China’s rising
goods surplus should be ignored and the Fund’s sole
focus should be on policies that lead to internal

### Page 11

11
balance. That provides one rationale for the Fund’s
ongoing decision to essentially ignore the contentious
issue of the PBOC’s ongoing exchange rate
management.
That argument, however, assumes that China’s
economy is small and thus China’s external surplus
can safely be ignored because it isn’t a drag on the
growth of the IMF’s other members.
That clearly isn’t true.
And it isn’t clear that China is more interested in
having a negotiation over its level of basic pension

benefits or the size of its fiscal deficit (where the
IMF continues to make timid recommendations that
would not move the needle—and certainly wouldn’t
meaningfully reduce China’s external surplus) than a
discussion over its currency practices.****
Back in the world of fixed but adjustable exchange
rates, there was an expectation (not always met to be
sure) that surplus countries would allow their
currencies to strengthen to bring their economies

back into external balance, and that other policy tools
would be used to ensure internal balance.
I would argue that China’s exchange rate (against
both the dollar and the basket) has many similarities
to the old world of fixed but adjustable currencies.
True, it moves on a controlled path when it moves

### Page 12

12
between different levels rather than jumping up and
down. But the PBOC does frequently target a level,
and that targeted level does change over time.
I would further argue that steps like the recent tariff
rate quota that will limit China’s exports of hybrid
cars to Europe to around 350,000 units next year (up
from 2025, but well below the 800,000 units of
2026) aren’t going to address the underlying
imbalance on their own. They can provide a bit of a

buffer for specific sectors.
But that buffer, while necessary, won’t address the
broad-based erosion of manufacturing capacity
outside China. This erosion will persist as long as an
undervalued RMB sends the entire world a signal to
use China as its production base.
The new system of managed trade that is emerging in
autos would need to be progressively expanded to an
ever-increasing set of sectors if protection is used as a
substitute for both domestic reform in China and

exchange rate adjustment.
That is now the direction of travel, but it doesn’t
address the underlying systemic issues.
Incidentally, those who believe the path of internal
demand is critical to rebalancing and that exchange
rate moves are secondary need to explain how an
acceleration in domestic demand would, on its own,

### Page 13

13
lead China’s external accounts to adjust. One
important, stylized fact about China is that domestic
demand growth is no longer feeding over to import
growth (ex-gold, that is).
Bottom line: China, which needs a 1.5 percentage
point contribution from net exports every year, is on a
collision course with its trading partners without a
currency correction that provides the basis for more
balanced trade even in the absence of trade
restrictions.
And there isn’t a realistic path towards balance that

doesn’t involve a stronger RMB; there aren’t, as Paul
Krugman once put it, immaculate transfers.
* That downturn shows up in domestic deflationary
pressure, and weak job growth/high rates of (at least
youth) unemployment. China, in other words, isn’t in
“internal balance.” I really liked the report from

### Page 14

14
Kamakshya Trivedi and Hui Shan of Goldman Sachs
arguing China needs two tools to hit two targets
(internal and external balance).
** To be sure, rate differentials matter for exporter
conversion—but so does the expected movement of
the RMB. The market understands this better than
the Fund. The Chen and Suwanapruti (Goldman
Sachs) note uses a framework similar to mine to
describe the CNY’s moves in Q3:
“In our view, sizeable FX inflows have continued to support

RMB appreciation despite unfavorable interest rate
differentials, as strong export receipts and increased exporter
FX conversions have outweighed portfolio outflows. Our
estimated FX conversion ratio rose to 63% in August,
compared with around 45% in normal years, likely reflecting a
shift in market expectations from RMB depreciation to
appreciation.”
*** Š ef č ovi č  has jurisdiction over trade policy
(narrowly defined), but not broader macroeconomic

policies. Europe’s institutional architecture for
exchange rate coordination is not well-developed.
**** The current account surplus is no longer masked
to the same extent as in 2023 and 2024, as SAFE has
fiddled with its BoP methodology once again (per the
Federal Reserve) and the BoP goods surplus is no
longer significantly below the customs goods surplus.
Of course, there are still problems in the data—the
deficit in the investment income account most

### Page 1

Asia Trends

2026 年10 月 21 日

1/3 
インドネシア中銀、市場動揺も、政策運営は政府と市場の「板挟み」が続く 
～一部の利上げ観測に「ゼロ回答」、様々な政策目標実現へ難しい対応が続くと予想される～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ インドネシア中銀は 10 月 20～21 日の理事会で、政策金利のＢＩレートを４会合連続で
5.75％に据え置いた。MSCI が市場の透明性やガバナンスへの懸念を背景に 「スタンドアロー
ン市場」への格下げ案を示し、株安・ルピア安が進んだことで、一部では利上げ観測が浮上
していた。しかし、中銀は５～６月の累計100bp の利上げで物価安定には十分との認識を示
し、預金ファシリティ金利を 25bp 引き上げて 5.00％とするとともに、為替ヘッジ取引への
優遇措置を強化し、通貨安定と海外資金の流入促進を図る判断を示した。 
○ 一方で、 景気下支えや雇用創出に向けて、 預金準備率を11～12 月に段階的に引き下げ、 銀行
の貸出余力を拡大する方針を示した。原油高に伴うインフレ圧力や、米国の金融引き締めに
よるドル高圧力を警戒する一方、国内消費の底堅さや民間投資の持ち直し、外需の回復など
を踏まえ、2026 年の経済成長率見通しは「＋4.9～5.7％」との従来の見通しを据え置いた。 
○ 中銀を巡ってはここ数年、政策目標に物価・通貨の安定に加え、経済成長や雇用創出を支援
する役割が追加されてきたほか、政治介入に対する懸念もある。こうしたなか、先行きも政
府の景気下支え要請と金融市場の通貨安定要求との「板挟み」となり、様々な政策目標の実
現を目指す観点から、一段と難しい政策運営を迫られる状況が続くと予想される。 
【インドネシア中銀、市場の混乱で一部に政策金利の引き上げ観測も、４会合連続で据え置き】 
インドネシア銀行（中銀）は、10 月 20～21 日に開催した月例理事会において、政策金利である
ＢＩレートを４会合連続で 5.75％に据え置くことを決定した。一方で、預金ファシリティ金利を
25bp 引き上げ、5.00％とした。中銀は、５月の月例理事会でルピア相場の安定を目的に50bp の大
幅利上げを決定したものの、その後も金融市場ではルピア安の動きが収まらず、株式、債券も下落
するトリプル安に直面した。このため、中銀は６月９日に開催した週次理事会で 25bp の緊急利上
げを実施するとともに、ルピア相場の安定に向けた追加対策を発表した。さらに、６月の月例理事
会でも 25bp の追加利上げを実施し、約１ヵ月の間に累計 100bp もの利上げを行うなど、通貨安定
に向けて金融引き締めを強化した。その後は、ルピア相場が落ち着きを取り戻したことから、前回
の９月会合まで３会合連続で政策金利を据え置き、様子見姿勢を維持してきた。 
しかし、 足元では同国の金融市場が再び混乱に直面している。 きっかけは、 指数算出会社のMSCI

### Page 2

2/3 
が 10 月15 日、同国市場を「スタンドアローン市場」に格下げする案を示し、市場参加者との協議
を開始したことにある（注１）。MSCI は
１月、同国株式市場の情報開示に懸念を
表明し、透明性向上への取り組みが進ま
なければ、フロンティア市場に格下げす
る可能性を示唆した。これを受けて、当
局は最低浮動株比率の大幅引き上げな
ど、海外投資家の懸念解消に向けた対策
を打ち出し、市場改革を加速させる姿勢
を示した。しかし、その後も市場参加者
からは、協調的な取引行為への疑念が解
消されておらず、実効性のある法執行も
確認できないとの指摘が MSCI に寄せられた。また、フロンティア市場への格下げでは、問題の深
刻さが適切に反映されないとの意見も寄せられた。このため、MSCI は 2027 年４月末まで市場参加
者の意見を募り、 同年６月末までに最終判断を示すとした。 協議を通じて市場のガバナンスや機能
の実質的な改善を裏付ける明確な証拠が確認されない限り、 格下げを進める方針である。 この発表
を受けて、主要株価指数（ジャカルタ総合指数）は一段と下振れしており、ルピア相場も下落して
いる（図１） 。 
こうしたなか、金融市場においては、月例理事会を前に、中銀が政策金利の引き上げを含む市場
安定策を打ち出すとの見方が浮上した。また、中銀は今回、会合後の記者会見の開始時刻を前倒し
すると発表した。中銀では、７月にペリー前総裁が任期を２年弱残して突然辞任し、上級副総裁で
あったデストリ・ダマヤンティ氏が総裁代行を務めた後、９月２日に総裁へ正式就任した。今回の
月例理事会は、デストリ氏の正式就任後２回目の定例会合に当たる。しかし、中銀は今回もＢＩレ
ートの引き上げを見送る一方、 預金ファシリティ金利の引き上げや為替ヘッジ取引への優遇措置の
強化を通じて、市場の安定を図る判断を示した。

【中銀は様々な政策目標の実現に向け、政府と市場の間で「板挟み」となることが予想される】 
会合後に公表した声明文では、 今回の決定について 「イラン情勢の緊迫化や米国の金融引き締め
による対外的な圧力が続くなかでのルピア相場の安定」、「2026 年と 2027 年の物価目標の達成」を
目的とする政策ミックスの一環との考えを示した。一方で、 景気下支えと国内の流動性確保に向け
て、 預金準備率の引き下げを決定した。 一般商業銀行向けは現行の９％から、11 月１日に８％、12
月１日に７％へ、 イスラム銀行向けは現行の7.5％から、11 月１日に6.5％、12 月１日に5.5％へ、
それぞれ段階的に引き下げる。さらに、為替ヘッジ取引への優遇措置を強化し、海外からの資金流

注１ 10 月16 日付レポート「インドネシア株に逆風、MSCI が「スタンドアローン」への格下げ検討」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666610.html）

### Page 3

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
3/3 
入を促す方針を示した。 
世界経済については「イラン情勢の悪化による原油価格の高止まりを受けて、 景気の下押しとイ
ンフレ圧力が併存している」との認識を示す一方、 「2026 年の世界経済成長率は＋３％となる」と
の従来見通しを維持した。 また、 「米国で追加利上げの可能性が高まっているうえ、 財政政策を理由
に米国債金利は高止まりしている」 として、 「新興国への資金流入が抑制され、 ドル高圧力が続く可
能性がある」との見通しを示した。 
一方、 同国経済について 「財政支援や消費者心理の改善を追い風に家計消費は底堅く推移してい
る」としつつ、足元では「企業マインドの改善を背景に民間投資に持ち直しの兆しがでている」と
の認識を示した。先行きは「外需の増加
も見込まれる」として、 「2026 年の経済
成長率は＋4.9～5.7％になる」との従来
見通しを据え置いた。そして、対外収支
も「世界的な不確実性の高まりの影響を
緩和すべく、国際収支を強化する必要が
ある」 としつつ、 「９月末時点の外貨準備
高は輸入の5.3 ヵ月分を有する」とし、
「2026 年の経常赤字はＧＤＰ比▲2.4～
▲1.6％」になると３会合ぶりに見通し
を示した。ルピア相場については「為替
介入やヘッジ取引への優遇措置などを通じた安定強化を図っている」 と従来からの対策を強化する
考えを示した。 そして、 物価動向については 「政府が設定した目標の範囲内で維持されている」 （図
２） ほか、 足元では 「天候不順による食料インフレの懸念が高まっている」ものの、「目標域内に収
めるべく、為替相場の安定に加え、政府も食料インフレ対策を強化する」との見方を示した。 
会合後に記者会見に臨んだ中銀のデストリ総裁は、MSCI による発表について 「株式市場の動向を
注視している」との見方を示した。そのうえで、政策運営について「的を絞ったものにしたい」と
して、 「海外からの投資の呼び込みに資するものにしたい」との考えを示した。そして、 「我々は５
月以降すでに100bp の利上げを実施しており、 物価安定には十分」 、 「海外投資家に対してインセン
ティブを提供している」との考えを示した。先行きの政策運営については「データ次第になる」と
して明言を避けた。また、ソリキン副総裁は、預金準備率引き下げの狙いについて「雇用創出を目
的としたもの」 、 「銀行の貸し出し余力の拡大を目指したもの」と説明した。そして、今回の政策対
応について「様々な政策目的に対応することを目指したもの」と説明した。 
こうした発言の背景には、ここ数年、中銀に対して、物価と通貨の安定に加え、経済成長や雇用
創出を支援する役割も求められるようになっていることがあると考えられる。 政治介入が懸念され
る動きもみられるなか、中銀の政策運営はこれまで以上に難しくなっている。先行きも、中銀は政
府からの景気下支え要請と、金融市場からの通貨安定要求との「板挟み」に置かれるとともに、難
しい政策運営を迫られる局面が続くと予想される。                  以 上

Report source: DLRI

### Page 1

Asia Trends

2026 年10 月 16 日

1/3 
インドネシア株に逆風、MSCI が「スタンドアローン」への格下げ検討 
～新興国市場からの除外を検討、市場の透明性とガバナンスに向けた改革の行方が焦点に～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ 2026 年のインドネシア株式市場は、 情報開示や市場の透明性を巡る懸念を背景に低迷が続い
ている。MSCI が１月末に同国株の指数組み入れ比率引き下げやフロンティア市場への格下げ
の可能性を示唆したことをきっかけに、FTSE ラッセルやＳ＆Ｐダウ・ジョーンズ・インデッ
クスも相次いで懸念を表明した。当局は最低浮動株比率の引き上げなど市場改革を進めたも
のの、協調的な取引への疑念や情報開示、法執行の実効性を巡る問題は解消されていない。 
○ こうしたなか、MSCI は 10 月 15 日、 インドネシアを新興国市場から 「スタンドアローン市場」
に再分類する案について市場参加者との協議を開始すると発表した。2027 年４月までに市場
のガバナンスや機能改善を示す明確な証拠が確認されなければ、再分類が進められる可能性
がある。実現すればパッシブ投資家を中心に資金流出が加速し、株式市場やルピア相場への
下押し圧力が一段と強まることが懸念される。格付機関も政策決定の信頼性低下を警戒する
なか、プラボウォ政権による実務重視への軌道修正と市場の信頼回復が今後の焦点となる。

2026 年のインドネシア株式市場は低迷している（図１） 。きっかけは、指数算出会社のMSCI が１
月末に株式市場の情報開示に懸念を表明
したことにある。同社は、情報開示の透
明性向上に向けた取り組みが進まなけれ
ば、同社が算出する世界株指数などへの
同国株の組み入れ比率 を引き下げ るほ
か、フロンティア市場に格下げする可能
性を示唆した。これを受けて、 同業のFTSE
ラッセルも定例で実施を予定していた同
国株指数の見直し延期を発表した。この
ため、主要株価指数（ジャカルタ総合指
数）は、格下げによるパッシブ投資家を
中心とする資金流出への警戒から調整の動きを強めた。

### Page 2

2/3 
その後、 当局は最低浮動株比率の大幅引き上げなど、 海外投資家の懸念解消に向けた対策を矢継
ぎ早に打ち出し、 市場改革を加速させる姿勢を示した。これを受けて、MSCI は当局の改革姿勢に一
定の評価を示したものの、審査の延長を決定するなど、最終判断を持ち越した。そのうえで、特定
株主による保有割合が高い銘柄を同社が算出するMSCI インドネシア指数、MSCI インドネシア小型
株指数から除外した。その後も、価格形成を歪める協調的な取引の兆候、英語での詳細な市場情報
の提供が不十分であること、 当局が公表した市場改革の一貫した持続的な取り組みが必要となるこ
となどを指摘し、MSCI は審査を再延長することを明らかにした。 
さらに、７月にはＳ＆Ｐダウ・ジョーンズ・インデックスが、株式市場の透明性に関する問題を
理由に、 新興国市場からフロンティア市場に格下げする可能性を警告した。 具体的には、同国を 2027
年の市場区分見直しの対象となる可能性のある監視リストに加えた。 同社は、 当局による情報開示
に関する懸念のほか、市場の流動性への潜在的な影響や保有構造の透明性向上に向けた取り組みを
注視するとした。状況が悪化した場合には、同国の証券に対する特別措置の導入を検討する必要が
あるとし、特別措置の導入から１年経過しても問題が解決しない場合には、 市場区分の見直しを実
施するとした。FTSE ラッセルは８月、同国株指数の見直し作業を再び延期する決定を下したため、
各社がどのような評価を下すかが注目されていた。 
こうしたなか、MSCI は10 月 15 日、 同国を新興国市場に分類することはもはや適切でないと判断
し、 「スタンドアローン市場」 に再分類する方針について、市場参加者からの意見募集を行うことを
明らかにした。 その理由について、フロンティア市場への再分類では市場の問題の深刻さが適切に
反映されないとの市場参加者からの意見が寄せられていることを明らかにした。また、 当局による
市場改革の発表以降も、 協調的な取引行為への疑念や、 実効性のある法執行の証拠がみられないこ
となど、市場の機能不全を巡る重大な懸念が残っているとした。今後、同社は 2027 年４月まで市
場参加者との協議を行い、市場のガバナンスと機能の改善を示す明確な証拠が確認されない限り、
再分類を進めるとした。同社は、市場へのアクセスや規模、流動性が著しく悪化した場合にスタン
ドアローンへの再分類を行っており、直近では 2021 年にアルゼンチン、2022 年にロシアを再分類
している。 
仮にインドネシアが再分類されれば、 パッシブ投資家を中心に資金流出の動きが一段と加速する
ことが予想されるなど、 年明け以降低迷が続く市場環境が厳しさを増すことは避けられない。 同国
を巡っては、 主要格付機関であるムーディーズとフィッチが、 政策決定の信頼性が低下しているこ
とを理由に、将来的なソブリン格付けの格下げにつながり得る見通しの引き下げ（ネガティブ）を
行うなど、金融市場から厳しい目が向けられている。プラボウォ政権は９月以降、財務相や外相が
相次いで交代し、実務重視に軌道修正する動きをみせている（注１） 。 しかし、 その成否はプラボウ
ォ大統領が専門的な判断をどこまで尊重するかに掛かっており、 低迷が続くルピア相場の行方にも

注１ 10 月13 日付レポート「インドネシア、財務相に続いて外相も交代、実務重視への軌道修正か」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666465.html）

### Page 3

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。
